(\Theta(\cdot),\Theta^{0}(\cdot))
.
Then the solution
\widehat{V}(\cdot\,,\cdot)
of the extended HJB equation (
3.11
) and the equilibrium control law
\widehat{u}(\cdot\,,\cdot)
can be given by

\widehat{V}(t,x)=\Theta^{0}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x)),\quad\widehat{u}(t,x)=\bar{\Psi}(t,x),\quad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(3.16)

Proof.

By the definition (
3.13
) of the operator
{\bf A}^{u}
, we have

({\bf A}^{u}k)(t,x)=k_{t}(t,x)+k_{x}(t,x)b(t,x,u)+\hbox{tr$\,$}[k_{xx}(t,x)a(t,x,u)],\quad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n},

for any
k(\cdot\,,\cdot)\in C^{1,2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R})
.
Then

\displaystyle({\bf A}^{u}\widehat{V})(t,x)-({\bf A}^{u}\widehat{f})(t,x,x)+({\bf A}^{u}\widehat{f}^{x})(t,x)-{\bf A}^{u}(\widehat{G}\diamond\widehat{g})(t,x)+({\bf H}^{u}\widehat{g})(t,x)

\displaystyle\quad=\widehat{V}_{t}(t,x)+\widehat{V}_{x}(t,x)b(t,x,u)+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\widehat{V}_{xx}(t,x)a(t,x,u)]-\big\{\widehat{f}_{\tilde{x}}(t,x,x)b(t,x,u)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\hbox{tr$\,$}[\widehat{f}_{x\tilde{x}}(t,x,x)a(t,x,u)]+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\widehat{f}_{\tilde{x}\tilde{x}}(t,x,x)a(t,x,u)]\big\}-\big\{\widehat{G}_{\tilde{x}}(x,\widehat{g}(t,x))b(t,x,u)

\displaystyle\qquad+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\widehat{G}_{\tilde{x}\tilde{x}}(x,\widehat{g}(t,x))a(t,x,u)]+2\hbox{tr$\,$}[\widehat{G}_{\tilde{x}y}(x,\widehat{g}(t,x))\widehat{g}_{x}(t,x)a(t,x)]

\displaystyle\qquad+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\widehat{G}_{yy}(x,\widehat{g}(t,x))\widehat{g}_{x}(t,x)^{2}a(t,x)]\big\},

(3.17)

where

\displaystyle\hbox{tr$\,$}[\widehat{G}_{\tilde{x}y}(x,\widehat{g}(t,x))\widehat{g}_{x}(t,x)a(t,x)]=\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{n}\widehat{G}_{\tilde{x}_{i}y_{j}}(x,\widehat{g}(t,x))\widehat{g}^{j}_{x_{k}}(t,x)\sigma^{i}(t,x)\sigma^{k}(t,x),

\displaystyle\hbox{tr$\,$}[\widehat{G}_{yy}(x,\widehat{g}(t,x))\widehat{g}_{x}(t,x)^{2}a(t,x)]=\sum_{i,j,k,l=1}^{n}\widehat{G}_{y_{i}y_{j}}(x,\widehat{g}(t,x))\widehat{g}^{i}_{x_{l}}(t,x)\widehat{g}^{j}_{x_{k}}(t,x)\sigma^{l}(t,x)\sigma^{k}(t,x).

Let

\bar{V}(t,x)=\Theta^{0}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x)),\quad\bar{u}(t,x)=\bar{\Psi}(t,x),\quad\bar{g}(t,x)=\Theta(t,x),\quad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n},

(3.18)

and

\bar{f}(t,\tilde{x},x)=\Theta^{0}(t,\tilde{x},x,y)-\widehat{G}(\tilde{x},y),\quad(t,\tilde{x},x,y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(3.19)

We note that in the above, we use the fact that
\Theta^{0}(t,\tilde{x},x,y)-\widehat{G}(\tilde{x},y)
is independent of
y
.
Then it is clearly seen that

{\bf A}^{\bar{u}}\bar{g}(t,x)=0,\quad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n},\quad\bar{g}(T,x)=x,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

and

{\bf A}^{\bar{u}}\bar{f}^{z}(t,x)=0,\quad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n},\quad\bar{f}^{z}(T,x)=\widehat{F}(z,x).

Note that
\Theta^{0}_{y}(s,\tilde{x},x,y)=\widehat{G}_{y}(\tilde{x},y)
.
From (
3.17
), by some straightforward calculations we get

\displaystyle({\bf A}^{u}\bar{V})(t,x)-({\bf A}^{u}\bar{f})(t,x,x)+({\bf A}^{u}\bar{f}^{x})(t,x)-{\bf A}^{u}(\widehat{G}\diamond\bar{g})(t,x)+({\bf H}^{u}\bar{g})(t,x)

\displaystyle\quad=\Theta^{0}_{t}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))+\Theta^{0}_{y}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))\Theta_{t}(t,x)

\displaystyle\qquad+\big\{\Theta^{0}_{x}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))+\Theta^{0}_{\tilde{x}}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))+\Theta^{0}_{y}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))\Theta_{x}(t,x)\big\}b(t,x,u)

\displaystyle\qquad+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta^{0}_{xx}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))a(t,x,u)]+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta^{0}_{y}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))\Theta_{xx}(t,x)a(t,x,u)]

\displaystyle\qquad+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta^{0}_{\tilde{x}\tilde{x}}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))a(t,x,u)]+2\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta^{0}_{x\tilde{x}}(t,x,x\Theta(t,x))a(t,x,u)]

\displaystyle\qquad-\big\{\bar{f}_{\tilde{x}}z(t,x,x)b(t,x,u)+2\hbox{tr$\,$}[\bar{f}_{x\tilde{x}}(t,x,x)a(t,x,u)]+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\bar{f}_{\tilde{x}\tilde{x}}(t,x,x)a(t,x,u)]\big\}

\displaystyle\qquad-\big\{\widehat{G}_{\tilde{x}}(x,\Theta(t,x))b(t,x,u)+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\widehat{G}_{\tilde{x}\tilde{x}}(x,\Theta(t,x))a(t,x,u)]\big\}

\displaystyle\quad=\Theta^{0}_{t}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))+\Theta^{0}_{x}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))b(t,x,u)+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta^{0}_{xx}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))a(t,x,u)]

\displaystyle\qquad+\Theta^{0}_{y}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))\big\{\Theta_{t}(t,x)+\Theta_{x}(t,x)b(t,x,u)+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta_{xx}(t,x)a(t,x,u)]\big\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\big\{\Theta^{0}_{\tilde{x}}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))-\bar{f}_{\tilde{x}}(t,x,x)-\bar{G}_{\tilde{x}}(x,\Theta(t,x))\big\}b(t,x,u)

\displaystyle\qquad+\hbox{tr$\,$}\big\{[\Theta_{\tilde{x}\tilde{x}}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))-\bar{f}_{\tilde{x}\tilde{x}}(t,x,x)-\bar{G}_{\tilde{x}\tilde{x}}(x,\Theta(t,x))]a(t,x,u)\big\}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta_{x\tilde{x}}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))a(t,x,u)]-2\hbox{tr$\,$}[\bar{f}_{x\tilde{x}}(t,x,x)a(t,x,u)]

\displaystyle\quad=\Theta^{0}_{t}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))+\Theta^{0}_{x}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))b(t,x,u)+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta^{0}_{xx}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))a(t,x,u)]

\displaystyle\qquad+\Theta^{0}_{y}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))\big\{\Theta_{t}(t,x)+\Theta_{x}(t,x)b(t,x,u)+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta_{xx}(t,x)a(t,x,u)]\big\}.

(3.20)

Then from (
3.15
) and (
3.18
), we get

\displaystyle({\bf A}^{\bar{u}}\bar{V})(t,x)-({\bf A}^{\bar{u}}\bar{f})(t,x,x)+({\bf A}^{\bar{u}}\bar{f}^{x})(t,x)-{\bf A}^{\bar{u}}(\widehat{G}\diamond\bar{g})(t,x)+({\bf H}^{\bar{u}}\bar{g})(t,x)

\displaystyle\quad=\inf_{u\in U}\big\{({\bf A}^{u}\bar{V})(t,x)-({\bf A}^{u}\bar{f})(t,x,x)+({\bf A}^{u}\bar{f}^{x})(t,x)-{\bf A}^{u}(\widehat{G}\diamond\bar{g})(t,x)+({\bf H}^{u}\bar{g})(t,x)\big\}.

Taking
u=\bar{u}
in (
3.20
) and then by (
3.14
), we get

\displaystyle\inf_{u\in U}\big\{({\bf A}^{u}\bar{V})(t,x)-({\bf A}^{u}\bar{f})(t,x,x)+({\bf A}^{u}\bar{f}^{x})(t,x)-{\bf A}^{u}(\widehat{G}\diamond\bar{g})(t,x)+({\bf H}^{u}\bar{g})(t,x)\big\}=0.

This completes the proof.
∎

Compared with Björk–Khapko–Murgoci
[
7
]
,
our approach has the following advantages.

\bullet

The equilibrium value function given by
V(t,x)\equiv\Theta^{0}(t,x,x,\Theta(t,x))
,

\Theta^{0}(\cdot\,,\tilde{x},\cdot\,,y)
can be regarded as an auxiliary function with parameters
(\tilde{x},y)
.
By introducing this auxiliary function,
the structure of equilibrium HJB equations is much clearer than that of extended HJB equations
(compare (
3.14
) with (
3.11
), for example), and
the meaning of the two PDEs in equilibrium HJB equations is also very clear (see
2.9
).

\bullet

The state term
X(T)
and the conditional expectation term
\mathbb{E}_{t}[X(T)]
in the terminal cost of (
1.3
) could be inseparable,
while in
[
7
]
, they are required to be separable.
The reason is that in our approach, we do not need to introduce a PDE to give an additional representation for

\widehat{G}(X(t),\mathbb{E}_{t}[X(T)])
.
Thus, there are only two PDEs in the equilibrium HJB equation, while the extended HJB equation (
3.11
) is involved with three PDEs.

\bullet

More importantly, by
Theorem 2.10
the well-posedness of equilibrium HJB equations is established under assumption
(H4)
,
while there is no rigorous argument about the well-posedness of the extended HJB equation (
3.11
) given in
[
7
]
.
More generally, the problem studied in the paper can depend on a controlled backward process and
have a recursive cost functional, which is determined by a BSVIE.
In Subsections
5.2
and
5.3
, two examples are presented to show that
the adoption of backward controlled processes is necessary in some important applications.

3.4
Comparison with Peng
[
50
]
and Yong
[
74
]

Under different assumptions on the control domain
U
,
Peng
[
50
]
and Yong
[
74
]
studied the Pontryagin’s maximum principle for
the optimal control problem of FBSDE (
1.1
)
with the Bolza type cost functional (
1.6
).
This provides a probabilistic approach to the optimal control of Problem (N).
Next, we are going to formally derive the “HJB equation” associated with the value function,
which can be regarded as a PDE approach version of
[
50
,
74
]
.
By this approach, we can see how the backward state process
(Y(\cdot),Z(\cdot))
in the running cost
affects the time-consistency and the “HJB equation” of the problem.

We assume that the value function
V(\cdot\,,\cdot)
is smooth, and

h^{0}(r,\tilde{x},x,y)=h^{0}(x),\qquad g^{0}(r,s,\tilde{x},x,u,y,z,y^{0},z^{0})=g^{0}(s,x,u,y,z).

(3.21)

Suppose the problem has an optimal control
u^{*}(\cdot)
with the closed-loop representation
u^{*}(s)=\Psi^{*}(s,X^{*}(s))
.
For any closed-loop strategy
\Psi(\cdot\,,\cdot)
, with the state
X(\cdot)
, we have

\displaystyle V(t,x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big\{h(X(T))+\int_{t}^{T}g^{0}(s,X(s),\Psi(s,X(s)),Y(s),Z(s))ds

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t}^{T}\Big[V_{s}(s,X(s))+V_{x}(s,X(s))b(s,X(s),\Psi(s,X(s)))+\hbox{tr$\,$}[V_{xx}(s,X(s))a(s,X(s),\Psi(s,X(s)))]

\displaystyle\quad+g^{0}(s,X(s),\Psi(s,X(s)),Y(s),Z(s))\Big]\Big\}ds

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big\{h(X(T))+\int_{t}^{T}g^{0}\big(s,X(s),\Psi(s,X(s)),\Theta^{\Psi}(s,X(s)),\Theta_{x}^{\Psi}(s,X(s))\sigma(s,X(s),\Psi(s,X(s)))\big)ds

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t}^{T}\Big[V_{s}(s,X(s))+V_{x}(s,X(s))b(s,X(s),\Psi(s,X(s)))+\hbox{tr$\,$}[V_{xx}(s,X(s))a(s,X(s),\Psi(s,X(s)))]

\displaystyle\quad+g^{0}\big(s,X(s),\Psi(s,X(s)),\Theta^{\Psi}(s,X(s)),\Theta_{x}^{\Psi}(s,X(s))\sigma(s,X(s),\Psi(s,X(s)))\big)\Big]ds\Big\},

with

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Theta^{\Psi}_{s}(s,x)+\Theta^{\Psi}_{x}(s,x)b(s,x,\Psi(s,x))+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta^{\Psi}_{xx}(s,x)a(s,x,\Psi(s,x))]\\
&\quad+g\big(s,x,\Psi(s,x),\Theta^{\Psi}(s,x),\Theta_{x}^{\Psi}(s,x)\sigma(s,x,\Psi(s,x))\big)=0,\\
&\Theta^{\Psi}(T,x)=h(x).\end{aligned}\right.

(3.22)

Note that

V(t,x)=J(t,x;\Psi^{*}(\cdot,\cdot))\leqslant J(t,x;\Psi(\cdot,\cdot)).

By the optimality,
\Psi^{*}(\cdot\,,\cdot)
should satisfy

\displaystyle V_{s}(s,x)+V_{x}(s,x)b(s,x,\Psi^{*}(s,x))+\hbox{tr$\,$}[V_{xx}(s,x)a(s,X(s),\Psi^{*}(s,x))]

\displaystyle\quad+g^{0}\big(s,x,\Psi^{*}(s,x),\Theta^{\Psi^{*}}(s,x),\Theta_{x}^{\Psi^{*}}(s,x)\sigma(s,x,\Psi^{*}(s,x))\big)=0,

(3.23)

and

\displaystyle V_{x}(s,x)b(s,x,\Psi^{*}(s,x))+\hbox{tr$\,$}[V_{xx}(s,x)a(s,x,\Psi^{*}(s,x))]

\displaystyle+g^{0}\big(s,x,\Psi^{*}(s,x),\Theta^{\Psi^{*}}(s,x),\Theta_{x}^{\Psi^{*}}(s,x)\sigma(s,x,\Psi^{*}(s,x))\big)

\displaystyle\quad=\inf_{\Psi(\cdot,\cdot)}\Big[V_{x}(s,x)b(s,x,\Psi(s,x))+\hbox{tr$\,$}[V_{xx}(s,x)a(s,x,\Psi(s,x))]

\displaystyle\qquad+g^{0}\big(s,x,\Psi(s,x),\Theta^{\Psi}(s,x),\Theta_{x}^{\Psi}(s,x)\sigma(s,x,\Psi(s,x))\big)\Big].

(3.24)

On the other hand, under (
3.21
), the equilibrium HJB equation (
2.18
)–(
2.19
) reads

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Theta_{s}(s,x)+\Theta_{x}(s,x)b(s,x,\bar{\Psi}(s,x))+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta_{xx}(s,x)a(s,x,\bar{\Psi}(s,x))]\\
&\quad+g\big(s,x,\bar{\Psi}(s,x),\Theta(s,x),\Theta_{x}(s,x)\sigma(s,x,\bar{\Psi}(s,x))\big)=0,\quad(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n},\\
&\Theta^{0}_{s}(s,x)+\Theta^{0}_{x}(s,x)b(s,x,\bar{\Psi}(s,x))+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta^{0}_{xx}(s,x)a(s,x,\bar{\Psi}(s,x))]\\
&\quad+g^{0}\big(s,x,\bar{\Psi}(s,x),\Theta(s,x),\Theta_{x}(s,x)\sigma(s,x,\bar{\Psi}(s,x))\big)=0,\quad(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n},\\
&\Theta(T,x)=h(x),\quad\Theta^{0}(T,x)=h^{0}(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\end{aligned}\right.

(3.25)

where

\begin{array}[]{ll}\bar{\Psi}(s,x)=\psi\big(s,x,\Theta(s,x),\Theta_{x}(s,x),\Theta_{xx}(s,x),\Theta^{0}(s,x),\Theta^{0}_{x}(s,x),\Theta^{0}_{xx}(s,x)\big),\qquad(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n},\end{array}

(3.26)

with
\psi(\cdot)
being determined by (
2.16
).
The equilibrium strategy
\bar{\Psi}(\cdot,\cdot)
satisfies the following local optimality condition:

\displaystyle\Theta^{0}_{x}(s,x)b(s,x,\bar{\Psi}(s,x))+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta^{0}_{xx}(s,x)a(s,x,\bar{\Psi}(s,x))]+g^{0}\big(s,x,\bar{\Psi}(s,x),\Theta(s,x),\Theta_{x}(s,x)\sigma(s,x,\bar{\Psi}(s,x))\big)

\displaystyle\quad=\inf_{u\in U}\Big[\Theta^{0}_{x}(s,x)b(s,x,u)+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta^{0}_{xx}(s,x)a(s,x,u)]+g^{0}\big(s,x,u,\Theta(s,x),\Theta_{x}(s,x)\sigma(s,x,u)\big)\Big].

(3.27)

It is remarkable that if the terminal cost does not depend on
y
,
the associated equilibrium HJB equation (
3.25
) is reduced
to an
(m+1)
-dimensional fully nonlinear parabolic equation without nonlocal terms.
By comparing (
3.22
)–(
3.24
) and (
3.25
)–(
3.26
),
we see that the difference between the optimal strategy and the equilibrium strategy
mainly lies on the corresponding optimality conditions (
3.24
) and (
3.26
).
Note that at
(s,x)
,
\Theta^{\Psi}(s,x)
depends on the values of
\Psi(\cdot\,,\cdot)
on
[s,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
(
3.24
) is not an optimality condition in the finite dimensional space
U
and

\Psi^{*}(\cdot\,,\cdot)
has an aftereffect.
Because of this, Problem (N) is time-inconsistent and the usual DPP/HJB approach does not work.

4
Linear-Quadratic Problems

Consider the controlled linear FBSDEs:

\left\{\negthinspace\negthinspace\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle dX(s)=[A(s)X(s)+B(s)u(s)]ds+[C(s)X(s)+D(s)u(s)]dW(s),\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle dY(s)=-\big[\widehat{A}(s)X(s)+\widehat{B}(s)u(s)+\widehat{C}(s)Y(s)+\widehat{D}(s)Z(s)\big]ds+Z(s)dW(s),\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle X(t)=x,\qquad Y(T)=HX(T).\end{array}\right.

(4.1)

We introduce the following cost functional:

\displaystyle{\cal J}(t,x;u(\cdot))\negthinspace=\negthinspace{1\over 2}\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big\{\negthinspace\int_{t}^{T}\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\big[\langle Q(s)X(s),X(s)\rangle\negthinspace+\negthinspace\langle M(s)Y(s),Y(s)\rangle\negthinspace+\negthinspace\langle N(s)Z(s),Z(s)\rangle\negthinspace+\negthinspace\langle R(s)u(s),u(s)\rangle\big]ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\langle G_{1}X(T),X(T)\rangle+\langle G_{2}Y(t),Y(t)\rangle+\langle G_{3}X(t),Y(t)\rangle+2\langle g,X(T)\rangle\Big\}.

(4.2)

The above problem is referred to as a
linear-quadratic
(LQ,
for short) optimal control problem for FBSDEs, due to the linearity of the
state equation (
4.1
) and the quadratic form of the cost functional (
4.2
).
For simplicity, we shall denote the optimal control problem with state equation (
4.1
)
and cost functional (
4.2
) by Problem (FBLQ).
We refer
[
41
,
63
,
32
,
64
,
40
,
28
,
58
,
61
]

again for some related results of the LQ control problems for FBSDEs/BSDEs.

Remark 4.1
.

Note that in the cost functional (
4.2
), we introduce a cross term
\langle G_{3}X(t),Y(t)\rangle
.
In the literature, the dependence of initial states is motivated by the so-called

state-dependent risk aversions
in finance
(see Björk–Murgoci–Zhou
[
9
]
).
Indeed, the initial state
X(t)
, with a form of
\langle X(t),Y(t)\rangle
, will also arise naturally when we study
the leader’s problem of an LQ Stackelberg game (see
[
59
, Subsection 3.2]
).

Let us take a look at a special case of the above LQ problem.

Example 4.2
.

Let
m=n=1
;
A,D\equiv 0
,
C,B\equiv 1
;
\widehat{A},\widehat{B},\widehat{C},\widehat{D}\equiv 0
,
H=1
; and
Q,M,N\equiv 0
,
R\equiv 2I
,
G_{1}=0
,
G_{2}=2I
,
G_{3}=0
,
g=0
.
Note that
Y(t)=\mathbb{E}_{t}[X(T)]
.
Then the state equation (
4.1
) and the cost functional (
4.2
) are reduced to

\left\{\negthinspace\negthinspace\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle dX(s)=u(s)ds+X(s)dW(s),\quad s\in[t,T],\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle X(t)=x,\end{array}\right.

and

J(t,x;u(\cdot))=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big[\int_{t}^{T}|u(s)|^{2}ds+|\mathbb{E}_{t}[X(T)]|^{2}\Big].

By Yong
[
76
]
, the unique optimal control
\bar{u}(\cdot;t,x)
with initial pair
(t,x)
is given by

\bar{u}(s;t,x)=-{x\over T-t+1},\quad s\in[t,T].

Then the optimal state process
\bar{X}(\cdot)\equiv\bar{X}(\cdot\,;t,x)
is given by

\bar{X}(s)=e^{-{1\over 2}(s-t)+W(s)-W(t)}x+{x\over T-t+1}\int_{t}^{s}e^{-{1\over 2}(s-r)+W(s)-W(r)}dr,\quad s\in[t,T].

For any
\tau\in(t,T)
, the optimal control with initial pair
(\tau,\bar{X}(\tau))
is given by

\bar{u}(s;\tau,\bar{X}(\tau))=-{\bar{X}(\tau)\over T-\tau+1},\quad s\in[\tau,T].

Thus, on
[\tau,T]
,

\bar{u}(\cdot\,;t,x)\neq\bar{u}(\cdot\,;\tau,\bar{X}(\tau)),

which implies that the problem is time-inconsistent.

From the above example and
1.1
,
we see that the LQ optimal control problem for FBSDEs is also time-inconsistent in general.
Recently, an LQ problem for coupled FBSDEs was studied by Hu–Ji–Xue
[
28
]
,
in which, however, the time-consistency was not realized.
Thus the optimal control obtained in
[
28
]
is a pre-committed optimal control.

In the following, we will mainly look at the corresponding forms of our equilibrium HJB equations.
The well-posedness of the associated Riccati equation is left for our future research.
The associated equilibrium HJB equation reads

\left\{\negthinspace\negthinspace\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\Theta_{s}(s,x)+\Theta_{x}(s,x)[A(s)x+B(s)\bar{\Psi}(s,x)]\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\quad+{1\over 2}\langle\Theta_{xx}(s,x)[C(s)x+D(s)\bar{\Psi}(s,x)],\,C(s)x+D(s)\bar{\Psi}(s,x)\rangle\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\quad+\widehat{A}(s)x+\widehat{B}(s)\bar{\Psi}(s,x)+\widehat{C}(s)\Theta(s,x)+\widehat{D}(s)\Theta_{x}(s,x)[C(s)x+D(s)\bar{\Psi}(s,x)]=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\Theta^{0}_{s}(s,\tilde{x},x,y)+\Theta^{0}_{x}(s,\tilde{x},x,y)[A(s)x+B(s)\bar{\Psi}(s,x)]\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\quad+{1\over 2}\langle\Theta^{0}_{xx}(s,\tilde{x},x,y)[C(s)x+D(s)\bar{\Psi}(s,x)],\,C(s)x+D(s)\bar{\Psi}(s,x)\rangle\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\quad+{1\over 2}\big\{\langle Q(s)x,x\rangle+\langle M(s)\Theta(s,x),\Theta(s,x)\rangle+\langle N(s)\Theta_{x}(s,x)[C(s)x+D(s)\bar{\Psi}(s,x)],\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\qquad\Theta_{x}(s,x)[C(s)x+D(s)\bar{\Psi}(s,x)]\rangle+\langle R(s)\bar{\Psi}(s,x),\bar{\Psi}(s,x)\rangle\big\}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\Theta(T,x)=Hx,\quad\Theta^{0}(T,\tilde{x},x,y)={1\over 2}\langle G_{1}x,x\rangle+{1\over 2}\langle G_{2}y,y\rangle+{1\over 2}\langle G_{3}\tilde{x},y\rangle+\langle g,x\rangle,\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}(s,x)

\displaystyle=-[D^{\top}\Theta^{0}_{xx}(s,x,x,\Theta(s,x))D+R+D^{\top}\Theta_{x}(s,x)^{\top}N\Theta_{x}(s,x)D]^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\times\big\{[D^{\top}\Theta^{0}_{xx}(s,x,x,\Theta(s,x))C+D^{\top}\Theta_{x}(s,x)^{\top}N\Theta_{x}(s,x)C]x+B^{\top}\Theta^{0}_{x}(s,x,x,\Theta(s,x))

\displaystyle\quad+[B^{\top}\Theta_{x}(s,x)^{\top}+\widehat{B}^{\top}+D^{\top}\Theta_{x}(s,x)^{\top}\widehat{D}^{\top}]\Theta^{0}_{y}(s,x,x,\Theta(s,x)\big\}.

In the above, we have taken the ansatz
\Theta_{xx}(\cdot)\equiv 0
.
Now let us take the following ansatz for
\Theta^{0}(s,\tilde{x},x,y)
and
\Theta(s,x)
:

\displaystyle\Theta^{0}(s,\tilde{x},x,y)={1\over 2}\langle\Phi_{1}(s)x,x\rangle+{1\over 2}\langle\Phi_{2}(s)y,y\rangle+{1\over 2}\langle\Phi_{3}(s)\tilde{x},y\rangle+\Phi_{4}(s)x+\Phi_{5}(s),

\displaystyle\Theta(s,x)=\Phi_{6}(s)x+\Phi_{7}(s),

where
\Phi_{i}(\cdot)

(i=1,...,7)
are undetermined functions (of proper dimensions).
Then the equilibrium strategy is given by

\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}(s,x)

\displaystyle=-[D^{\top}\Phi_{1}D+R+D^{\top}\Phi_{6}^{\top}N\Phi_{6}D]^{-1}\Big\{D^{\top}\Phi_{1}C+D^{\top}\Phi_{6}^{\top}N\Phi_{6}C+B^{\top}\Phi_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+B^{\top}\Phi_{6}^{\top}\Phi_{2}\Phi_{6}+\widehat{B}^{\top}\Phi_{2}\Phi_{6}+D^{\top}\Phi_{6}^{\top}\widehat{D}^{\top}\Phi_{2}\Phi_{6}+{1\over 2}B^{\top}\Phi_{6}^{\top}\Phi_{3}+{1\over 2}\widehat{B}^{\top}\Phi_{3}+{1\over 2}D^{\top}\Phi_{6}^{\top}\widehat{D}^{\top}\Phi_{3}\Big\}x

\displaystyle\quad-[D^{\top}\Phi_{1}D+R+D^{\top}\Phi_{6}^{\top}N\Phi_{6}D]^{-1}\big\{B^{\top}\Phi_{4}+[B^{\top}\Phi_{6}^{\top}+\widehat{B}^{\top}+D^{\top}\Phi_{6}^{\top}\widehat{D}^{\top}]\Phi_{2}\Phi_{7}\big\}

\displaystyle=:\bar{\Psi}(s)x+\bar{v}(s),

(4.3)

where
\Phi_{i}(\cdot)
is determined by the following
Riccati-type
ordinary differential equation
(ODE, for short):

\left\{\negthinspace\negthinspace\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{1}+\Phi_{1}(A+B\bar{\Psi})+(A+B\bar{\Psi})^{\top}\Phi_{1}+(C+D\bar{\Psi})^{\top}\Phi_{1}(C+D\bar{\Psi})\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\quad+Q+\Phi_{6}^{\top}M\Phi_{6}+(C+D\bar{\Psi})^{\top}\Phi_{6}^{\top}N\Phi_{6}(C+D\bar{\Psi})+\bar{\Psi}^{\top}R\bar{\Psi}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{2}=0,\quad\dot{\Phi}_{3}=0,\quad\dot{\Phi}_{4}+\bar{v}^{\top}B^{\top}\Phi_{1}+\Phi_{4}(A+B\bar{\Psi})+\bar{v}^{\top}D^{\top}\Phi_{1}(C+D\bar{\Psi})\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\quad+\Phi_{7}^{\top}M\Phi_{6}+\bar{v}^{\top}\Phi_{6}^{\top}N\Phi_{6}(C+D\bar{\Psi})+\bar{v}^{\top}R\bar{\Psi}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{5}+\Phi_{4}B\bar{v}+{1\over 2}\bar{v}^{\top}D^{\top}\Phi_{1}D\bar{v}+{1\over 2}\bar{v}^{\top}\Phi_{6}^{\top}N\Phi_{6}\bar{v}+{1\over 2}\bar{v}^{\top}R\bar{v}+{1\over 2}\Phi_{7}^{\top}M\Phi_{7}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{6}+\Phi_{6}(A+B\bar{\Psi})+\widehat{A}+\widehat{B}\bar{\Psi}+\widehat{C}\Phi_{6}+\widehat{D}\Phi_{6}(C+D\bar{\Psi})=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{7}+\Phi_{6}B\bar{v}+\widehat{B}\bar{v}+\widehat{C}\Phi_{7}+\widehat{D}\Phi_{6}D\bar{v}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(T)=G_{1},\quad\Phi_{2}(T)=G_{2},\quad\Phi_{3}(T)=G_{3},\quad\Phi_{4}(T)=g,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\Phi_{5}(T)=0,\quad\Phi_{6}(T)=H,\quad\Phi_{7}(T)=0.\end{array}\right.

(4.4)

Proposition 4.3
.

Suppose that the Riccati equation (
4.4
) admits a unique solution.
Then the strategy
\bar{\Psi}(\cdot,\cdot)
given by (
4.3
)
is an equilibrium strategy of
Problem (FBLQ)
.

When the weighting matrices
G_{3}=0
and
g=0
, then
\Phi_{i}\equiv 0
for
i=3,4,5,7
,
and the Riccati equation (
4.4
) can be simplified.
The following result shows that the LQ problem for FBSDEs is closely related to
the so-called
mean-field LQ optimal control problems
(see
[
76
,
78
,
60
]
, for example).
Let
\widehat{A},\,\widehat{B},\,\widehat{C},\,\widehat{D},\,M,\,N\equiv 0
,
G_{3}=0
,
g=0
and
H=I_{n}
.
Then the state equation (
4.1
) and the cost functional (
4.2
) are reduced to

\left\{\begin{aligned} dX(s)&=[A(s)X(s)+B(s)u(s)]ds+[C(s)X(s)+D(s)u(s)]dW(s),\\
X(t)&=x,\end{aligned}\right.

and

\displaystyle J(t,x;u(\cdot))

\displaystyle={1\over 2}\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big\{\int_{t}^{T}\big[\langle Q(s)X(s),X(s)\rangle+\langle R(s)u(s),u(s)\rangle\big]ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\langle G_{1}X(T),X(T)\rangle+\langle G_{2}\mathbb{E}_{t}[X(T)],\mathbb{E}_{t}[X(T)]\rangle\Big\},

respectively. The associated Riccati equation (
4.4
) reads

\left\{\negthinspace\negthinspace\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{1}+\Phi_{1}(A+B\bar{\Psi})+(A+B\bar{\Psi})^{\top}\Phi_{1}+(C+D\bar{\Psi})^{\top}\Phi_{1}(C+D\bar{\Psi})+Q+\bar{\Psi}^{\top}R\bar{\Psi}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{2}=0,\quad\dot{\Phi}_{6}+\Phi_{6}(A+B\bar{\Psi})=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(T)=G_{1},\quad\Phi_{2}(T)=G_{2},\quad\Phi_{6}(T)=I_{n},\end{array}\right.

(4.5)

with

\bar{\Psi}=-[D^{\top}\Phi_{1}D+R]^{-1}[D^{\top}\Phi_{1}C+B^{\top}\Phi_{1}+B^{\top}\Phi_{6}^{\top}\Phi_{2}\Phi_{6}].

(4.6)

Denote
\Phi=\Phi_{1}
and
\widehat{\Phi}=\Phi_{1}+\Phi_{6}^{\top}\Phi_{2}\Phi_{6}
.
Then we can rewrite (
4.5
)–(
4.6
) as follows:

\left\{\negthinspace\negthinspace\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}+\Phi(A+B\bar{\Psi})+(A+B\bar{\Psi})^{\top}\Phi+(C+D\bar{\Psi})^{\top}\Phi(C+D\bar{\Psi})+Q+\bar{\Psi}^{\top}R\bar{\Psi}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\widehat{\Phi}}+\widehat{\Phi}(A+B\bar{\Psi})+(A+B\bar{\Psi})^{\top}\widehat{\Phi}+(C+D\bar{\Psi})^{\top}\Phi(C+D\bar{\Psi})+Q+\bar{\Psi}^{\top}R\bar{\Psi}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\Phi(T)=G_{1},\quad\widehat{\Phi}(T)=G_{1}+G_{2},\end{array}\right.

(4.7)

with

\bar{\Psi}=-[D^{\top}\Phi D+R]^{-1}[D^{\top}\Phi C+B^{\top}\widehat{\Phi}].

(4.8)

We emphasize that (
4.7
) is exactly a special case of the Riccati-type equation derived by Yong
[
78
]
.
Thus, under some positivity conditions, one can obtain
the well-posedness of (
4.7
) from
[
78
, Theorem 4.6]
easily. The well-posedness of Riccati equation (
4.4
) will be published separately.

5
Applications

In this section, we shall investigate three important applications, which are also the main motivations
of studying forward-backward optimal control problems mentioned in Introduction.

5.1
Mean-variance Models

Consider a Black–Scholes market model in which there is one bond with the riskless interest rate
r>0
and one stock with the appreciation rate
\mu>0
and volatility
\sigma>0
. Then a standard argument leads to the following SDE for the wealth process
X(\cdot)
:

\left\{\negthinspace\negthinspace\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle dX(s)=[rX(s)+(\mu-r)u(s)]ds+\sigma u(s)dW(s),\quad s\in[t,T],\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle X(t)=\xi,\end{array}\right.

(5.1)

where
u(\cdot)
is the dollar amount invested in the stock.
The investor wishes to minimize the following functional:

J(t,\xi;u(\cdot))=-\mathbb{E}_{t}[X(T)]+{\gamma\over 2}\hbox{var$\,$}_{t}[X(T)]=-\mathbb{E}_{t}[X(T)]+{\gamma\over 2}\mathbb{E}_{t}[|X(T)|^{2}]-{\gamma\over 2}|\mathbb{E}_{t}[X(T)]|^{2}.

(5.2)

It is known (see Basak–Chabakauri
[
3
]
) that the optimal control of the above mean-variance model is time-inconsistent.
We shall apply
Theorem 2.7
to find a time-consistent equilibrium.

Note that the cost functional (
5.2
) can be rewritten as

J(t,\xi;u(\cdot))=-\mathbb{E}_{t}[X(T)]+{\gamma\over 2}\mathbb{E}_{t}[|X(T)|^{2}]-{\gamma\over 2}|Y(t)|^{2},

with

\left\{\negthinspace\negthinspace\begin{array}[]{ll}\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle dX(s)=[rX(s)+(\mu-r)u(s)]ds+\sigma u(s)dW(s),\quad s\in[t,T],\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle dY(s)=Z(s)dW(s),\quad s\in[t,T],\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle X(t)=\xi,\quad Y(T)=X(T).\end{array}\right.

Thus, the mean-variance model is a special case of the linear-quadratic problems for FBSDEs.
By
4.3
, the equilibrium strategy
\bar{\Psi}(\cdot,\cdot)
can be given by

\bar{\Psi}(t,x)=\bar{\Psi}(s)x+\bar{v}(s),\quad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R},

where

\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}(s)=-[\sigma^{2}\Phi_{1}]^{-1}\big[(\mu-r)\Phi_{1}+(\mu-r)\Phi_{2}\Phi^{2}_{6}+{1\over 2}(\mu-r)\Phi_{3}\Phi_{6}\big],

\displaystyle\bar{v}(s)=-[\sigma^{2}\Phi_{1}]^{-1}\big[(\mu-r)\Phi_{4}+(\mu-r)\Phi_{6}\Phi_{2}\Phi_{7}\big],

with

\left\{\negthinspace\negthinspace\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{1}+2\Phi_{1}(r+(\mu-r)\bar{\Psi})+\sigma\bar{\Psi}\Phi_{1}\sigma\bar{\Psi}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{2}=0,\quad\dot{\Phi}_{3}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{4}+\bar{v}(\mu-r)\Phi_{1}+\Phi_{4}(r+(\mu-r)\bar{\Psi})+\bar{v}\sigma\Phi_{1}\sigma\bar{\Psi}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{5}+\Phi_{4}(\mu-r)\bar{v}+{1\over 2}\bar{v}\sigma\Phi_{1}\sigma\bar{v}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{6}+\Phi_{6}(r+(\mu-r)\bar{\Psi})=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{7}+\Phi_{6}(\mu-r)\bar{v}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(T)=\gamma,\quad\Phi_{2}(T)=-\gamma,\quad\Phi_{3}(T)=0,\quad\Phi_{4}(T)=-1,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\Phi_{5}(T)=0,\quad\Phi_{6}(T)=1,\quad\Phi_{7}(T)=0.\end{array}\right.

From the above, it is easily seen that
\Phi_{2}\equiv-\gamma
,
\Phi_{3}\equiv 0
and
\Phi_{1}+\Phi_{6}\Phi_{2}\Phi_{6}\equiv 0
. Thus,

\bar{\Psi}=0,\quad\bar{v}=-[\sigma^{2}\Phi_{1}]^{-1}\big[(\mu-r)\Phi_{4}-(\mu-r)\Phi_{6}\gamma\Phi_{7}\big],

(5.3)

and

\left\{\negthinspace\negthinspace\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{1}+2r\Phi_{1}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{4}-\sigma^{-2}(\mu-r)^{2}\big[\Phi_{4}-\Phi_{6}\gamma\Phi_{7}\big]\Phi_{1}+r\Phi_{4}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{6}+r\Phi_{6}=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{7}-\Phi_{6}(\mu-r)[\sigma^{2}\Phi_{1}]^{-1}\big[(\mu-r)\Phi_{4}-(\mu-r)\Phi_{6}\gamma\Phi_{7}\big]=0,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(T)=\gamma,\quad\Phi_{4}(T)=-1,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(T)=1,\quad\Phi_{7}(T)=0.\end{array}\right.

(5.4)

By first solving the unknown variables
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{6}
, equation (
5.4
) becomes a linear equation. By the variation of
constants formula, the unique solution
(\Phi_{1},\Phi_{4},\Phi_{6},\Phi_{7})
of equation (
5.4
) can be explicitly solved. Then the equilibrium strategy can be given by (
5.3
). Indeed, we can observe that

{d[\Phi_{4}-\Phi_{6}\gamma\Phi_{7}\big]\over dt}=-\gamma[\Phi_{4}-\Phi_{6}\gamma\Phi_{7}\big],\quad[\Phi_{4}-\Phi_{6}\gamma\Phi_{7}\big](T)=-1,

which implies that

[\Phi_{4}-\Phi_{6}\gamma\Phi_{7}\big]=-e^{-\gamma(t-T)}.

Substituting the above and
\Phi_{1}(t)=e^{-2\gamma(t-T)}
into (
5.3
), the equilibrium strategy
\bar{\Psi}(\cdot,\cdot)
is explicitly given by

\bar{\Psi}(t,x)={\mu-r\over\gamma\sigma^{2}}e^{-r(t-T)},\quad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}.

From the above, we see that the optimal control problem of FBSDEs is a natural extension of the conditional mean-variance problem,
with the dynamic risk measure
\hbox{var$\,$}\negthinspace_{t}[X(T)]
replaced by some more general ones,
which can be described by some process
Y(\cdot)
satisfying a BSDE.
We refer the reader to Riedel
[
54
]
, Barrieu–El Karoui
[
2
]
and Detlefsen–Scandolo
[
15
]

for the theory of risk measures.
Mathematically, the dynamic risk measure
Y(t)
could depend on the whole path
X(s);t\leqslant s\leqslant T
of the state
X(\cdot)
,
while
\hbox{var$\,$}\negthinspace_{t}[X(T)]
is only affected by the terminal value
X(T)
. Moreover, Problem (N) can also be regarded as an extension of
the dynamic mean-variance models with the conditional expectation operator
\mathbb{E}_{t}[\,\cdot\,]
replaced by the so-called
g-expectation
operator
{\cal E}_{g,t}[\,\cdot\,]
,
which was introduced by Peng
[
51
]
and then was widely applied in finance; see Chen–Epstein
[
12
]
,
Coquet
[
13
]
and Chen–Chen–Davison
[
11
]
, for example.

5.2
Social Planner Problems
with Heterogeneous Epstein-Zin Utilities

In this subsection, we shall consider a social planner problem for Merton’s investment-consumption models,
in which each agent’s objective is given by an Epstein–Zin utility.
The social planner would like to maximize the utility of the coalition,
which is a convex combination of each agent’s utility.
The main feature of our model is that the discount rate in each agent’s utility can be different.
We will reveal two interesting facts: (1) the model is time-inconsistent;
(ii) the situation of controlled backward state equations is not avoidable in this model.

Consider the following SDE for the wealth process
X(\cdot)
:

\left\{\negthinspace\negthinspace\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle dX(s)=\big\{rX(s)+(\mu-r)[u_{1}(s)+u_{2}(s)]-[c_{1}(s)+c_{2}(s)]\big\}ds+\sigma\big[u_{1}(s)+u_{2}(s)\big]dW(s),\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle X(t)=\xi,\end{array}\right.

where
u_{i}(\cdot)
and
c_{i}(\cdot)
are the dollar amount invested in the stock and the consumption of agent
i
(
i
=1,2), respectively.
Naturally, agent
i
wants to maximize his/her utility functional

J_{i}(t,\xi;u_{1}(\cdot),u_{2}(\cdot),c_{1}(\cdot),c_{2}(\cdot))=Y_{i}(t),

where
Y_{i}(\cdot)
, called an Epstein–Zin utility (see
[
17
,
20
]
, for example),
is determined by

Y_{i}(s)=\mathbb{E}_{s}\Big[\int_{s}^{T}g_{i}(c_{1}(r)+c_{2}(r),Y_{i}(r))dr+h_{i}(X(T))\Big],\quad s\in[t,T],

(5.5)

with

g_{i}(c,y)=\alpha^{-1}((1-\gamma)y)^{1-{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}[c^{\alpha}-\rho_{i}((1-\gamma)y)^{{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}],\quad h_{i}(x)={x^{1-\gamma}\over 1-\gamma}.

The parameter
\gamma>0
controls the
risk aversion of the agents,
{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}>0
gives the agents’ IES,
and
\rho_{i}
is the discount rate of agent
i
(which could be different for different
i
).

Such kind of models was initially studied by Duffie–Geoffard–Skiadas
[
19
]

(also see Ma–Yong
[
43
, Page 6]
),
however, the time-inconsistency issue was not realized.
If the agents decide to cooperate, then the social planer would try to maximize

\displaystyle J^{\lambda}(t,\xi;u_{1}(\cdot),u_{2}(\cdot),c_{1}(\cdot),c_{2}(\cdot))

\displaystyle\quad=\lambda J_{1}(t,\xi;u_{1}(\cdot),u_{2}(\cdot),c_{1}(\cdot),c_{2}(\cdot))+(1-\lambda)J_{2}(t,\xi;u_{1}(\cdot),u_{2}(\cdot),c_{1}(\cdot),c_{2}(\cdot)),

where
\lambda\in[0,1]
is a weighting parameter of the two agents.
Denote
c(\cdot)=c_{1}(\cdot)+c_{2}(\cdot)
and
u(\cdot)=u_{1}(\cdot)+u_{2}(\cdot)
.
Then the state equation and the utility functional of the social planner (or called the group decision-maker) become

\left\{\begin{aligned} dX(s)&=[rX(s)+(\mu-r)u(s)-c(s)]ds+\sigma u(s)dW(s),\\
dY_{i}(s)&=-g_{i}(c(s),Y_{i}(s))ds+Z_{i}(s)dW(s);\quad i=1,2,\\
X(t)&=\xi,\quad Y_{i}(T)=h_{i}(X(T)),\end{aligned}\right.

(5.6)

and

\displaystyle J^{\lambda}(t,\xi;u(\cdot),c(\cdot))\equiv J^{\lambda}(t,\xi;u_{1}(\cdot),u_{2}(\cdot),c_{1}(\cdot),c_{2}(\cdot))

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big\{{X(T)^{1-\gamma}\over 1-\gamma}+\int_{t}^{T}\Big[\lambda\alpha^{-1}((1-\gamma)Y_{1}(r))^{1-{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}\big(c(r)^{\alpha}-\rho_{1}((1-\gamma)Y_{1}(r))^{{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+(1-\lambda)\alpha^{-1}((1-\gamma)Y_{2}(r))^{1-{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}\big(c(r)^{\alpha}-\rho_{2}((1-\gamma)Y_{2}(r))^{{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}\big)\Big]dr\Big\}.

(5.7)

Remark 5.1
.

Note that when
\alpha=1-\gamma
, the Epstein–Zin utility
Y_{i}(\cdot)
is reduced to the standard
constant relative risk aversion utility case,
because the corresponding BSDE (
5.5
) is linear with respect to the unknown process
Y_{i}(\cdot)
.
Then the corresponding utility (
5.7
) becomes

\displaystyle J^{\lambda}(t,\xi;u(\cdot),c(\cdot))

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big\{\big[\lambda e^{-\rho_{1}(T-t)}+(1-\lambda)e^{-\rho_{2}(T-t)}\big]{X(T)^{\alpha}\over\alpha}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\int_{t}^{T}\big[\lambda e^{-\rho_{1}(r-t)}+(1-\lambda)e^{-\rho_{2}(r-t)}\big]c(r)^{\alpha}dr\Big\}.

(5.8)

The control problem with state equation (
5.6
) and utility functional (
5.8
)
is exactly the Merton’s problem with a quasi-exponential discounting function
\lambda e^{-\rho_{1}\cdot}+(1-\lambda)e^{-\rho_{2}\cdot}
.
We refer the reader to
[
22
,
21
,
44
,
45
,
75
,
10
]
for
more results on this special case. In the general case, that is
\alpha
could not equal
1-\gamma
,
the Epstein–Zin utility
Y_{i}(\cdot)
is described by the solution to a nonlinear BSDE.
Then the situation of controlled BSDEs is not avoidable.

It is clearly seen that the control problem with state equation (
5.6
) and utility functional (
5.7
) is time-inconsistent.
Thus, the group decision-maker should look for an equilibrium strategy for the coalition.
The associated equilibrium HJB equation reads

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Theta^{1}_{t}(t,x)+\Theta^{1}_{x}(t,x)[rx+(\mu-r)\mathbb{U}(t,x)-\mathbb{C}(t,x)]+{1\over 2}\Theta^{1}_{xx}(t,x)[\sigma\mathbb{U}(t,x)]^{2}\\
&\qquad+\alpha^{-1}((1-\gamma)\Theta^{1}(t,x))^{1-{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}[\mathbb{C}(t,x)^{\alpha}-\rho_{1}((1-\gamma)\Theta^{1}(t,x))^{{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}]=0;\\
&\Theta^{2}_{t}(t,x)+\Theta^{2}_{x}(t,x)[rx+(\mu-r)\mathbb{U}(t,x)-\mathbb{C}(t,x)]+{1\over 2}\Theta^{2}_{xx}(t,x)[\sigma\mathbb{U}(t,x)]^{2}\\
&\qquad+\alpha^{-1}((1-\gamma)\Theta^{2}(t,x))^{1-{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}[\mathbb{C}(t,x)^{\alpha}-\rho_{2}((1-\gamma)\Theta^{2}(t,x))^{{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}]=0;\\
&\Theta^{0}_{t}(t,x)+\Theta^{0}_{x}(t,x)[rx+(\mu-r)\mathbb{U}(t,x)-\mathbb{C}(t,x)]+{1\over 2}\Theta^{0}_{xx}(t,x)[\sigma\mathbb{U}(t,x)]^{2}\\
&\qquad+\lambda\alpha^{-1}((1-\gamma)\Theta^{1}(t,x))^{1-{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}[\mathbb{C}(t,x)^{\alpha}-\rho_{1}((1-\gamma)\Theta^{1}(t,x))^{{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}]\\
&\qquad+(1-\lambda)\alpha^{-1}((1-\gamma)\Theta^{2}(t,x))^{1-{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}[\mathbb{C}(t,x)^{\alpha}-\rho_{2}((1-\gamma)\Theta^{2}(t,x))^{{\alpha\over 1-\gamma}}]=0;\\
&\Theta^{1}(T,x)={x^{1-\gamma}\over 1-\gamma},\quad\Theta^{2}(T,x)={x^{1-\gamma}\over 1-\gamma},\quad\Theta^{0}(T,x)={x^{1-\gamma}\over 1-\gamma},\end{aligned}\right.

(5.9)

with the equilibrium investment strategy:

\mathbb{U}(t,x)={(r-\mu)\Theta^{0}_{x}(t,x)\over\sigma^{2}\Theta^{0}_{xx}(t,x)},

(5.10)

and the equilibrium consumption strategy:

\mathbb{C}(t,x)={\Theta^{0}_{x}(t,x)^{1\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda((1-\gamma)\Theta^{1}(t,x))^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)((1-\gamma)\Theta^{2}(t,x))^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{1\over\alpha-1}}.

(5.11)

Let us make the ansatz:

\displaystyle\Theta^{1}(t,x)={1\over 1-\gamma}x^{1-\gamma}\theta_{1}(t),\quad\Theta^{2}(t,x)={1\over 1-\gamma}x^{1-\gamma}\theta_{2}(t),

\displaystyle\Theta^{0}(t,x)={\theta_{0}(t)\over 1-\gamma}x^{1-\gamma}={\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)\over 1-\gamma}x^{1-\gamma}.

Then

\displaystyle\Theta^{i}_{x}(t,x)=\theta_{i}(t)x^{-\gamma},\quad\Theta^{i}_{xx}(x)=-\gamma\theta_{i}(t)x^{-\gamma-1},

\displaystyle\Theta^{0}_{x}(t,x)=\lambda\theta_{1}(t)x^{-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)x^{-\gamma},

\displaystyle\Theta^{0}_{xx}(x)=-\lambda\gamma\theta_{1}(t)x^{-\gamma-1}-(1-\lambda)\gamma\theta_{2}(t)x^{-\gamma-1}.

The equilibrium investment strategy (
5.10
) and the equilibrium consumption strategy (
5.11
) become

\displaystyle\mathbb{U}(t,x)

\displaystyle={(\mu-r)\over\gamma\sigma^{2}}x,\quad x\in\mathbb{R},

(5.12)

and

\mathbb{C}(t,x)={[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{1\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{1\over\alpha-1}}x,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

(5.13)

with

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dot{\theta}_{1}(t)+(1-\gamma)\theta_{1}(t)\Big[r+{(\mu-r)^{2}\over 2\gamma\sigma^{2}}-{[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{1\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{1\over\alpha-1}}\Big]\\
&\qquad-(1-\gamma)\rho_{1}\alpha^{-1}\theta_{1}(t)+{\alpha^{-1}(1-\gamma)\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{\alpha\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{\alpha\over\alpha-1}}=0;\\
&\dot{\theta}_{2}(t)+(1-\gamma)\theta_{2}(t)\Big[r+{(\mu-r)^{2}\over 2\gamma\sigma^{2}}-{[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{1\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{1\over\alpha-1}}\Big]\\
&\qquad-(1-\gamma)\rho_{2}\alpha^{-1}\theta_{2}(t)+{\alpha^{-1}(1-\gamma)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{\alpha\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{\alpha\over\alpha-1}}=0;\\
&\theta_{1}(T)=\theta_{2}(T)=1.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.14)

Proposition 5.2
.

The system (
5.14
) of ODEs admits a unique solution
(\theta_{1}(\cdot),\theta_{2}(\cdot))
.
The strategies
\mathbb{U}(\cdot,\cdot)
and
\mathbb{C}(\cdot,\cdot)
, given by (
5.12
)–(
5.13
),
is an equilibrium investment strategy and an equilibrium consumption strategy, respectively.

Proof.

It suffices to show that if
(\theta_{1}(\cdot),\theta_{2}(\cdot))
is a postive solution of (
5.14
)
on
[t_{0},T]
, then

{\delta}\leqslant\theta_{i}(s)\leqslant\kappa,\quad s\in[t_{0},T];\quad i=1,2,

for some positive constants
\delta,\kappa>0
independent of
t_{0}
.
Without loss of generality, let
\rho_{1}\geqslant\rho_{2}
.
Denote
\Delta\theta(\cdot)=\theta_{1}(\cdot)-\theta_{2}(\cdot)
.
Note that

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Delta\dot{\theta}(t)+(1-\gamma)\Delta\theta(t)\Big[r+{(\mu-r)^{2}\over 2\gamma\sigma^{2}}-{[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{1\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{1\over\alpha-1}}\Big]\\
&\qquad-(1-\gamma)\rho_{1}\alpha^{-1}\Delta\theta(t)+(1-\gamma)(\rho_{2}-\rho_{1})\alpha^{-1}\theta_{2}(t)\\
&\qquad+{\alpha^{-1}(1-\gamma-\alpha)[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{\alpha\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{\alpha\over\alpha-1}}\int_{0}^{1}[l\theta_{1}(t)+(1-l)\theta_{2}(t)]^{-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}dl\Delta\theta(t)=0,\\
&\Delta\theta(T)=0.\end{aligned}\right.

and

(1-\gamma)(\rho_{2}-\rho_{1})\alpha^{-1}\theta_{2}(t)\leqslant 0.

Then,
\theta_{1}(\cdot)\leqslant\theta_{2}(\cdot)
.
Thus,

\displaystyle{\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{\alpha\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{\alpha\over\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle\quad={\theta_{1}(t)\theta_{1}(t)^{-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)][\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{1\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}][\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{1\over\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle\quad={\theta_{1}(t)[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{1}(t)^{-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}\theta_{2}(t)][\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{1\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}][\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{1\over\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle\quad\geqslant{\theta_{1}(t)[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}][\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{1\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}][\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{1\over\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle\quad={\theta_{1}(t)[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{1\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{1\over\alpha-1}},

which implies

\displaystyle(1-\gamma)\theta_{1}(t)\Big[-{[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{1\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{1\over\alpha-1}}\Big]

\displaystyle+{\alpha^{-1}(1-\gamma)\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{\alpha\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{\alpha\over\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle\quad\geqslant{(\alpha^{-1}-1)(1-\gamma)\theta_{1}(t)[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{1\over\alpha-1}\over[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{1\over\alpha-1}}\geqslant 0.

If follows that

\theta_{1}(t)\geqslant e^{\int_{t}^{T}(1-\gamma)[r-\rho_{1}\alpha^{-1}+{(\mu-r)^{2}\over 2\gamma\sigma^{2}}]ds}\geqslant e^{-T|1-\gamma||r-\rho_{1}\alpha^{-1}+{(\mu-r)^{2}\over 2\gamma\sigma^{2}}|}=:\delta>0.

By
\theta_{2}\geqslant\theta_{1}
, we get
\theta_{2}\geqslant\delta
. Then

\displaystyle\theta_{i}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}\leqslant\delta^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma};\,i=1,2,\quad[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)]^{\alpha\over\alpha-1}\leqslant\delta^{\alpha\over\alpha-1},

\displaystyle[\lambda\theta_{1}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}+(1-\lambda)\theta_{2}(t)^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over 1-\gamma}]^{1\over 1-\alpha}\leqslant\delta^{1-\gamma-\alpha\over(1-\alpha)(1-\gamma)}.

From the above, we get that there exists a constant
\kappa>0
, independent of
t_{0}
, such that

\theta_{i}\leqslant\kappa.

Then a routine argument applies to get the well-posedness of the equation.
∎

5.3
Stackelberg Games

In this subsection, we consider a specific Stackelberg game (also called a leader-follower game).
We will show that the leader’s problem in this Stackelberg game is an optimal control problem for FBSDEs,
whose optimal control is time-inconsistent. By applying
Theorem 2.7
,
we can find a time-consistent equilibrium for the leader. This will give a very good illustration.

Example 5.3
.

Consider the following one-dimensional state equation

\left\{\negthinspace\negthinspace\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\dot{X}(s)=u_{1}(s)-u_{2}(s),\quad s\in[t,1],\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle X(t)=x,\end{array}\right.

(5.15)

and the cost functionals

\displaystyle J_{1}(t,x;u_{1}(\cdot),u_{2}(\cdot))

\displaystyle=|X(1)|^{2}+\int_{t}^{1}\big[|u_{1}(s)|^{2}-|u_{2}(s)|^{2}\big]ds,

(5.16)

\displaystyle J_{2}(t,x;u_{1}(\cdot),u_{2}(\cdot))

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{1}\Big[-u_{1}(s)+{X(s)\over s-2}+u_{2}(s)+|u_{2}(s)|^{2}\Big]ds.

(5.17)

In the above, Player
2
is the leader (or the principal), who announces his/her control
u_{2}(\cdot)
first, and Player
1
is the follower (or the agent), who chooses his/her control
u_{1}(\cdot)
accordingly. Whatever the leader announces, the follower will select a control
\bar{u}_{1}(\cdot\,;t,x,u_{2}(\cdot))
(depending the control
u_{2}(\cdot)
announced by the leader as well as the initial pair
(t,x)
) such that
u_{1}(\cdot)\mapsto J_{1}(t,x;u_{1}(\cdot),u_{2}(\cdot))
is minimized.
Knowing this, the leader will choose a
\bar{u}_{2}(\cdot)
a priori so that
u_{2}(\cdot)\mapsto J_{2}(t,x;\bar{u}_{1}(\cdot\,;t,x,u_{2}(\cdot)),u_{2}(\cdot))
is minimized.
For any given initial pair
(t,x)
and control
u_{2}(\cdot)
of the leader,
by the standard results of LQ control problems (see
[
79
, Chapter 6]
),
the follower admits a unique optimal strategy
\bar{u}_{1}(\cdot\,;t,x,u_{2}(\cdot))
.
Then by some straightforward calculations, the leader’s problem can be stated as follows:
Find a control
u_{2}(\cdot)
to minimize

J_{2}(t,x;\bar{u}_{1}(\cdot\,;t,x,u_{2}(\cdot)),u_{2}(\cdot))=\int_{t}^{1}\big[Y(s)+u_{2}(s)+|u_{2}(s)|^{2}\big]ds,

(5.18)

with the backward evolution equation

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\dot{Y}(s)={1\over 2-s}Y(s)+{1\over 2-s}u_{2}(s),\quad s\in[t,1],\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\displaystyle Y(1)=0.\end{array}\right.

(5.19)

Note that

Y(s)={1\over s-2}\int_{s}^{1}u_{2}(r)dr.

Then

\displaystyle J_{2}(t,x;\bar{u}_{1}(\cdot),u_{2}(\cdot))

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{1}\Big[{1\over s-2}\int_{s}^{1}u_{2}(r)dr+u_{2}(s)+|u_{2}(s)|^{2}\Big]ds

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{1}\Big\{\big[\ln(2-s)-\ln(2-t)+1\big]u_{2}(s)+|u_{2}(s)|^{2}\Big\}ds.

It follows that the unique optimal control of the leader is given by

\bar{u}_{2}(s;t,x)={\ln(2-t)-\ln(2-s)-1\over 2},\quad s\in[t,1].

In particular, at the initial pair
(0,x)
, the unique optimal control of the leader is

\bar{u}_{2}(s;0,x)={\ln 2-\ln(2-s)-1\over 2},\quad s\in[0,1].

Let
\bar{X}(\cdot)\equiv\bar{X}(\cdot;0,x)
be the state process with initial pair
(0,x)

and optimal controls
(\bar{u}_{1}(s;t,x,\bar{u}_{2}),\,\bar{u}_{2}(\cdot))
.
For any given
t\in(0,1)
, at the initial pair
(t,\bar{X}(t;0,x))
, the unique optimal control of the leader is

\bar{u}_{2}(s;t,\bar{X}(t;0,x))={\ln(2-t)-\ln(2-s)-1\over 2},\quad s\in[t,1].

Thus, on the time interval
[t,1]
,

\bar{u}_{2}(\cdot;0,x)\neq\bar{u}_{2}(\cdot;t,\bar{X}(t;0,x)),

which implies that the leader’s problem is time-inconsistent.
By
Theorem 2.7
,
we can easily obtain the time-consistent equilibrium strategy of the leader,
which is given by

\bar{\Psi}(s,x)=-{1\over 2},\quad(s,x)\in[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Remark 5.4
.

We refer the reader to
[
56
,
72
,
14
,
4
,
59
]
for
some theoretical results and financial applications of Stackelberg games.
It is worthy of pointing out that the well-known
principal-agent problem
(see
[
14
]
)
can be regarded as a special case.

6
Verification Theorem

In this section, we shall show that the function
\bar{\Psi}(\cdot\,,\cdot)
, determined by (
2.19
),
is an equilibrium strategy of Problem (N).
In other words, we would like to rigorously prove the verification theorem
(i.e.,
Theorem 2.7
).
To do this, we assume that the equilibrium HJB equation (
2.18
)
admits a classical solution and the function
\bar{\Psi}(\cdot\,,\cdot)
defined by (
2.19
) is a feedback strategy.
We also assume that all the involved functions are bounded and differentiable with bounded derivatives.

Let
(\bar{X}(\cdot),\bar{Y}(\cdot),\bar{Z}(\cdot))
and
(\bar{Y}^{0}(\cdot),\bar{Z}^{0}(\cdot,\cdot))
be the solutions to FBSDE (
1.1
) and BSVIE (
1.3
),
respectively, corresponding to the strategy
\bar{\Psi}(\cdot\,,\cdot)
and the initial pair
(0,\xi)
.
For any
t\in[0,T)
,
u\in L_{{\cal F}_{t}}^{2}(\Omega;U)
and
\varepsilon\in[0,T-t)
,
define the strategy
\Psi^{\varepsilon}(\cdot\,,\cdot)
by (
2.15
).
With the initial pair
(t,\bar{X}(t))\in\mathscr{D}
, take the strategy
\Psi^{\varepsilon}(\cdot\,,\cdot)
,
then the corresponding state equation (
1.1
) and cost functional (
1.2
)–(
1.3
) become

\left\{\begin{aligned} dX^{\varepsilon}(s)&=b(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),\bar{\Psi}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s)))ds+\sigma(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),\bar{\Psi}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s)))dW(s),\quad s\in[t+\varepsilon,T];\\
dX^{\varepsilon}(s)&=b(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)ds+\sigma(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)dW(s),\quad s\in[t,t+\varepsilon),\\
dY^{\varepsilon}(s)&=-g(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),\bar{\Psi}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s)),Y^{\varepsilon}(s),Z^{\varepsilon}(s))ds+Z^{\varepsilon}(s)dW(s),\quad s\in[t+\varepsilon,T];\\
dY^{\varepsilon}(s)&=-g(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u,Y^{\varepsilon}(s),Z^{\varepsilon}(s))ds+Z^{\varepsilon}(s)dW(s),\quad s\in[t,t+\varepsilon),\\
X^{\varepsilon}(t)&=\bar{X}(t),\quad Y^{\varepsilon}(T)=h(X^{\varepsilon}(T)),\end{aligned}\right.

(6.1)

and

J(t,\bar{X}(t);\Psi^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))=Y^{0,\varepsilon}(t),

(6.2)

respectively, with

\left\{\begin{aligned} &Y^{0,\varepsilon}(r)=h^{0}(r,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(T),Y^{\varepsilon}(r))-\int_{r}^{T}Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s)dW(s)\\
&\quad+\int_{r}^{T}g^{0}(r,s,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\bar{\Psi}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s)),Y^{\varepsilon}(s),Z^{\varepsilon}(s),Y^{0,\varepsilon}(s),Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s))dr,\quad r\in[t+\varepsilon,T];\\
&Y^{0,\varepsilon}(r)=h^{0}(r,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(T),Y^{\varepsilon}(r))-\int_{r}^{T}Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s)dW(s)\\
&\quad+\int_{t+\varepsilon}^{T}g^{0}(r,s,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\bar{\Psi}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s)),Y^{\varepsilon}(s),Z^{\varepsilon}(s),Y^{0,\varepsilon}(s),Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s))ds\\
&\quad+\int_{r}^{t+\varepsilon}g^{0}(r,s,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(s),u,Y^{\varepsilon}(s),Z^{\varepsilon}(s),Y^{0,\varepsilon}(s),Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s))ds,\quad r\in[t,t+\varepsilon).\end{aligned}\right.

(6.3)

Next, let us deduce the PDEs associated with the FBSDE (
6.1
) and BSVIE (
6.3
).

By the Feynman–Kac formula for BSDEs (see Pardoux–Peng
[
49
]
, for example),
we get

\displaystyle Y^{\varepsilon}(s)=\Theta(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s)),\quad Z^{\varepsilon}(s)=\Theta_{x}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s))\sigma(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),\bar{\Psi}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s))),\quad s\in[t+\varepsilon,T],

where
\Theta(\cdot,\cdot)
is the unique solution to the first PDE in (
2.18
).
Then on the time interval
[t,t+\varepsilon]
, we can rewrite (
6.1
) as follows:

\left\{\begin{aligned} dX^{\varepsilon}(s)&=b(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)ds+\sigma(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)dW(s),\quad s\in[t,t+\varepsilon],\\
dY^{\varepsilon}(s)&=-g(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u,Y^{\varepsilon}(s),Z^{\varepsilon}(s))ds+Z^{\varepsilon}(s)dW(s),\quad s\in[t,t+\varepsilon],\\
X^{\varepsilon}(t)&=\bar{X}(t),\quad Y^{\varepsilon}(t+\varepsilon)=\Theta(t+\varepsilon,X^{\varepsilon}(t+\varepsilon)).\end{aligned}\right.

Note that the control
u\in L^{2}_{{\cal F}_{t}}(\Omega;U)
is
{\cal F}_{t}
-measurable.
Then by the Feynman–Kac formula for BSDEs again, we get

Y^{\varepsilon}(s)=\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s)),\quad Z^{\varepsilon}(s)=\Theta^{\varepsilon}_{x}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s))\sigma(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u),\quad s\in[t,t+\varepsilon],

(6.4)

where
\Theta^{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot)
is the unique classical solution to the following
perturbation
PDE:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Theta^{\varepsilon}_{s}(s,x)+\Theta^{\varepsilon}_{x}(s,x)b(s,x,u)+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta^{\varepsilon}_{xx}(s,x)a(s,x,u)]\\
&\quad+g(s,x,u,\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,x),\Theta^{\varepsilon}_{x}(s,x)\sigma(s,x,u))=0,\quad s\in[t,t+\varepsilon],\\
&\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t+\varepsilon,x)=\Theta(t+\varepsilon,x).\end{aligned}\right.

(6.5)

Remark 6.1
.

Indeed, (
6.5
) is a PDE with random parameters, because
u\in L^{2}_{{\cal F}_{t}}(\Omega;U)
is a random variable.
However, note that
u
is
{\cal F}_{t}
-measurable and (
6.5
) is considered only on
[t,t+\varepsilon]
.
The random PDE (
6.5
) can be treated as a deterministic one.

By
2.2
, on the time interval
[t+\varepsilon,T]
, we get

Y^{0,\varepsilon}(s)=\Theta^{0}(s,s,x,x,\Theta(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s))),

(6.6)

where
\Theta^{0}(\cdot)
is the solution of the second PDE in (
2.18
).
Motivated by Wang–Yong–Zhang
[
67
]
, we introduce the following auxiliary processes with two time variables:

\left\{\begin{aligned} dY^{0,\varepsilon}(r;s)&=-g^{0}\big(r,s,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\bar{\Psi}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s)),Y^{\varepsilon}(s),Z^{\varepsilon}(s),Y^{0,\varepsilon}(s),Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r;s)\big)ds\\
&\quad+Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r;s)dW(s),\quad s\in[(t+\varepsilon)\vee r,T],~r\in[t,T];\\
dY^{0,\varepsilon}(r;s)&=-g^{0}\big(r,s,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(s),u,Y^{\varepsilon}(s),Z^{\varepsilon}(s),Y^{0,\varepsilon}(s),Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r;s)\big)ds\\
&\quad+Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r;s)dW(s),\quad(r,s)\in\Delta^{*}[t,t+\varepsilon];\\
Y^{0,\varepsilon}(r;T)&=h^{0}(r,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(T),Y^{\varepsilon}(r)),\quad r\in[t,T],\end{aligned}\right.

(6.7)

which can give the unique solution of BSVIE (
6.3
) by

Y^{0,\varepsilon}(s)=Y^{0,\varepsilon}(s;s),\quad Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s)=Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r;s),\quad(r,s)\in\Delta^{*}[t,T].

(6.8)

Notice that for any fixed
r\in[t,T]
, (
6.7
) is a BSDE.
Recall the representations (
6.4
) and (
6.6
).
Then by the Feynman–Kac formula for BSDEs again, we get that for any
r\in[t,t+\varepsilon]
,

\displaystyle Y^{0,\varepsilon}(r;s)=\Theta^{0}(r,s,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(r,X^{\varepsilon}(r))),\quad s\in[t+\varepsilon,T],

\displaystyle Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r;s)=\Theta^{0}_{x}(r,s,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(r,X^{\varepsilon}(r)))\sigma(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),\bar{\Psi}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s))),\quad s\in[t+\varepsilon,T].

(6.9)

On the other hand, by the flow property of the auxiliary process
Y^{0,\varepsilon}(\cdot;\cdot)
, we have

\displaystyle Y^{0,\varepsilon}(r;r)

\displaystyle=Y^{0,\varepsilon}(r;t+\varepsilon)+\int_{r}^{t+\varepsilon}g^{0}(r,s,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(s),u,Y^{\varepsilon}(s),Z^{\varepsilon}(s),Y^{0,\varepsilon}(s),Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r;s))ds

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{r}^{t+\varepsilon}Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r;s)dW(s),\quad r\in[t,t+\varepsilon].

Substituting (
6.9
) into the above and noting (
6.8
), we get

\displaystyle Y^{0,\varepsilon}(r)

\displaystyle=\Theta^{0}(r,t+\varepsilon,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(t+\varepsilon),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(r,X^{\varepsilon}(r)))-\int_{r}^{t+\varepsilon}Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s)dW(s)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{r}^{t+\varepsilon}g^{0}(r,s,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(s),u,Y^{\varepsilon}(s),Z^{\varepsilon}(s),Y^{0,\varepsilon}(s),Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s))ds,\quad r\in[t,t+\varepsilon].

(6.10)

Then by
2.2
(recalling (
6.4
)–(
6.5
)),
we have the following representation:

\displaystyle Y^{0,\varepsilon}(r)=\Theta^{0,\varepsilon}(r,r,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(r),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(r,X^{\varepsilon}(r))),

\displaystyle Z^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s)=\Theta_{x}^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s,X^{\varepsilon}(r),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(r,X^{\varepsilon}(r)))\sigma(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u),\quad(r,s)\in\Delta^{*}[t,t+\varepsilon],

(6.11)

where
\Theta^{0,\varepsilon}(\cdot)
is the unique solution to the following PDE:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Theta_{s}^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s,\tilde{x},x,y)+\Theta_{x}^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s,\tilde{x},x,y)b(s,x,u)+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta_{xx}^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s,\tilde{x},x,y)a(s,x,u)]\\
&\quad+g^{0}\big(r,s,\tilde{x},x,u,\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,x),\Theta^{\varepsilon}_{x}(s,x)\sigma(s,x,u),\Theta^{0,\varepsilon}(s,s,x,x,\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,x)),\\
&\qquad\quad\Theta_{x}^{0,\varepsilon}(r,s,\tilde{x},x,y)\sigma(s,x,u)\big)=0,\quad(r,s)\in\Delta^{*}[t,t+\varepsilon],\\
&\Theta^{0,\varepsilon}(r,t+\varepsilon,\tilde{x},x,y)=\Theta^{0}(r,t+\varepsilon,\tilde{x},x,y),\quad r\in[t,t+\varepsilon].\end{aligned}\right.

(6.12)

Remark 6.2
.

Note that both (
6.5
) and (
6.12
) are semilinear parabolic equations.
To guarantee the well-posedness of PDEs (
6.5
) and (
6.12
),
we assume that the following non-degenerate condition holds:
There exist two constants
\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}>0
such that

\lambda_{0}I\leqslant a(t,x,u)\leqslant\lambda_{1}I,\quad\forall(t,x,u)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times U.

(6.13)

Under the assumption (
6.13
),
we have the following convergence result of the families
\{\Theta^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\}_{\varepsilon>0}
and
\{\Theta^{0,\varepsilon}(\cdot)\}_{\varepsilon>0}
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let (
6.13
) hold. Then the PDEs (
6.5
) and (
6.12
) admit unique classical solutions

\Theta^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)
and
\Theta^{0,\varepsilon}(\cdot)
, respectively.
Moreover, there exists a constant
K>0
, only depending on
\|\Theta(\cdot)\|_{C^{{\alpha\over 2},2+\alpha}}
and
\|\Theta^{0}(\cdot)\|_{C^{{\alpha\over 2},{\alpha\over 2},\alpha,\alpha,1+\alpha}}
, such that

\|\Theta^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)-\Theta(\cdot)\|_{C^{0,2}[t,t+\varepsilon]}+\|\Theta^{0,\varepsilon}(\cdot)-\Theta^{0}(\cdot)\|_{C^{0,0,0,0,1}[t,t+\varepsilon]}\leqslant K\varepsilon^{\alpha\over 2},

(6.14)

where
\alpha\in(0,1)
is a constant.

Remark 6.4
.

The proof of
6.3
is sketched in
Section 7
as a byproduct of
Theorem 2.10
.
We emphasize that for
6.3
, the assumption (
6.13
) should not be necessary,
because one could replace the analytic approach by a probabilistic argument
(see
[
49
,
67
]
).

Remark 6.5
.

The estimate (
6.14
) plays the same role as the convergence assumption (H3) in
Wei–Yong–Yu
[
70
]
, which was proved only for some special cases
(see
[
70
, Theorem 6.2]
).
In
6.3
, we can show that (
6.14
) holds in general.
The key point is that (
6.14
) is only a byproduct of the stability of semilinear parabolic equations,
while the assumption (H3) in
[
70
]
is concerned with the fully nonlinear PDEs.
The deeper reason is that in our paper the main technique is the Feynman–Kac formula for BSVIEs/BSDEs,
while in
[
70
]
they heavily rely on the HJB equation approach.

6.1
Proof of
Theorem 2.7

For any fixed
t\in[0,T)
,
\varepsilon\in[0,T-t]
and
u\in L_{{\cal F}_{t}}^{2}(\Omega;U)
,
let
\Theta^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)
and
\Theta^{0,\varepsilon}(\cdot)
be the unique classical solution to PDEs (
6.5
) and (
6.12
), respectively.
With the representations (
6.4
) and (
6.11
),
by (
6.10
) we can represent
Y^{0,\varepsilon}(t)
as follows:

\displaystyle Y^{0,\varepsilon}(t)=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big[\Theta^{0}(t,t+\varepsilon,X^{\varepsilon}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(t+\varepsilon),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,X^{\varepsilon}(t)))+\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}g^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s,u)ds\Big],

(6.15)

where

\displaystyle g^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s,u):=g^{0}\Big(t,\,s,\,X^{\varepsilon}(t),\,X^{\varepsilon}(s),\,u,\,\Theta^{\varepsilon}(X^{\varepsilon}(s),s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}_{x}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s))\sigma(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u),

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\Theta^{0,\varepsilon}(s,s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s))),\,\Theta_{x}^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s,X^{\varepsilon}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,X^{\varepsilon}(t)))\sigma(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)\Big).

(6.16)

Note that
X^{\varepsilon}(t)=\bar{X}(t)
.
Applying Itô’s formula to the mapping
s\mapsto\Theta^{0}(t,s,X^{\varepsilon}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,X^{\varepsilon}(t)))
yields that

\displaystyle Y^{0,\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big\{\Theta^{0}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))+\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\Big[\Theta_{s}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))

\displaystyle\qquad+\Theta_{x}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))b(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)+g^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s,u)

\displaystyle\qquad+\hbox{tr$\,$}\big[\Theta_{xx}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))a(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)\big]\Big]ds\Big\}.

Using the fact
\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t+\varepsilon,\cdot)=\Theta(t+\varepsilon,\cdot)
, we get

\displaystyle Y^{0,\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big\{\Theta^{0}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))-\Theta^{0}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t+\varepsilon,\bar{X}(t+\varepsilon)))

\displaystyle\qquad+\Theta^{0}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta(t+\varepsilon,\bar{X}(t+\varepsilon)))+\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\Big[\Theta_{s}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))

\displaystyle\qquad+\Theta_{x}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))b(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)+g^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s,u)

\displaystyle\qquad+\hbox{tr$\,$}\big[\Theta_{xx}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))a(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)\big]\Big]ds\Big\}.

Then by applying the Itô’s formula to the mapping

s\mapsto\Theta^{0}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s)))
, we have

\displaystyle Y^{0,\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big\{\Theta^{0}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta(t+\varepsilon,\bar{X}(t+\varepsilon)))

\displaystyle\qquad-{1\over 2}\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\hbox{tr$\,$}\Big[\Theta^{0}_{yy}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s)))\Theta_{x}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{\sigma}(s)[\Theta_{x}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{\sigma}(s)]^{\top}\Big]ds

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\Theta^{0}_{y}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s)))\Big[\Theta_{s}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))+\Theta_{x}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{b}(s)

\displaystyle\qquad+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta_{xx}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{a}(s)]\Big]ds+\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\Big[\Theta_{s}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))

\displaystyle\qquad+\Theta_{x}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))b(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)+g^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s,u)

\displaystyle\qquad+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta_{xx}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))a(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)]\Big]ds\Big\},

(6.17)

where

\displaystyle\bar{\varphi}(s):=\varphi(s,\bar{X}(s),\bar{\Psi}(s,\bar{X}(s))),\,\,s\in[t,t+\varepsilon],\quad\hbox{for}\quad\varphi(\cdot)=b(\cdot),\sigma(\cdot),a(\cdot).

(6.18)

Recalling (
6.5
) and (
2.18
), we get that on
[t,t+\varepsilon]
,

\displaystyle\Theta_{s}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))+\Theta_{x}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{b}(s)+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta_{xx}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{a}(s)]

\displaystyle\quad=\Theta_{x}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))[\bar{b}(s)-b(s,\bar{X}(s),u)]+\hbox{tr$\,$}\big\{\Theta_{xx}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))[\bar{a}(s)-a(s,\bar{X}(s),u)]\big\}-g^{\varepsilon}(s,u),

(6.19)

and

\displaystyle\Theta_{s}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))+\Theta_{x}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))b(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)

\displaystyle+\hbox{tr$\,$}[\Theta_{xx}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))a(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)]

\displaystyle\quad=\Theta_{x}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))[b(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)-\bar{b}^{\varepsilon}(s)]

\displaystyle\qquad+\hbox{tr$\,$}\big\{\Theta_{xx}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))[a(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)-\bar{a}^{\varepsilon}(s)]\big\}-\bar{g}^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s),

(6.20)

where

\displaystyle\bar{\varphi}^{\varepsilon}(s):=\varphi(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),\bar{\Psi}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s))),\quad\hbox{for}\quad\varphi(\cdot)=b(\cdot),\,\sigma(\cdot),\,a(\cdot);

\displaystyle g^{\varepsilon}(s,u):=g\big(s,\bar{X}(s),u,\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s)),\Theta^{\varepsilon}_{x}(s,\bar{X}(s))\sigma(s,\bar{X}(s),u)\big);

\displaystyle\bar{g}^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s):=g^{0}\big(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\bar{\Psi}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s)),\Theta(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s)),\Theta_{x}(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s))\bar{\sigma}^{\varepsilon}(s),

\displaystyle\quad\Theta^{0}(s,s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s))),\Theta_{x}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta(t,\bar{X}(t)))\bar{\sigma}^{\varepsilon}(s)\big).

(6.21)

Substituting (
6.19
) and (
6.20
) into (
6.17
) yields that

\displaystyle Y^{0,\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big\{\Theta^{0}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta(t+\varepsilon,\bar{X}(t+\varepsilon)))

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\Theta^{0}_{y}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s)))\Big[g^{\varepsilon}(s,u)+\Theta_{x}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times[b(s,\bar{X}(s),u)-\bar{b}(s)]+\hbox{tr$\,$}\big\{\Theta_{xx}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))[a(s,\bar{X}(s),u)-\bar{a}(s)]\big\}\Big]ds

\displaystyle\qquad-{1\over 2}\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\hbox{tr$\,$}\Big[\Theta^{0}_{yy}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s)))\Theta_{x}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{\sigma}(s)[\Theta_{x}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{\sigma}(s)]^{\top}\Big]ds

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\Big[g^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s,u)-\bar{g}^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s)+\Theta_{x}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))[b(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)-\bar{b}^{\varepsilon}(s)]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\hbox{tr$\,$}\big\{\Theta_{xx}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))[a(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)-\bar{a}^{\varepsilon}(s)]\big\}\Big]ds\Big\}.

(6.22)

Applying the above arguments to
\bar{Y}^{0}(t)
, we have

\displaystyle J(t,\bar{X}(t);\bar{\Psi}(\cdot))

\displaystyle\equiv\bar{Y}^{0}(t)=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big\{\Theta^{0}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta(t+\varepsilon,\bar{X}(t+\varepsilon)))

\displaystyle\quad-{1\over 2}\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\hbox{tr$\,$}\big\{\Theta^{0}_{yy}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta(s,\bar{X}(s)))\Theta_{x}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{\sigma}(s)[\Theta_{x}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{\sigma}(s)]^{\top}\big\}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\Theta^{0}_{y}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta(s,\bar{X}(s)))\bar{g}(s,\bar{\Psi}(s,\bar{X}(s)))ds\Big\},

(6.23)

where

\displaystyle\bar{g}(s,u)

\displaystyle:=g\big(s,\bar{X}(s),u,\Theta(s,\bar{X}(s)),\Theta_{x}(s,\bar{X}(s))\sigma(s,\bar{X}(s),u)\big),\quad(s,u)\in[t,t+\varepsilon]\times U.

(6.24)

Combining (
6.22
) with (
6.23
) together, we get

\displaystyle Y^{0,\varepsilon}(t)-\bar{Y}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big\{\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\Theta^{0}_{y}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s)))\Big[g^{\varepsilon}(s,u)+\Theta_{x}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))

\displaystyle\quad\times[b(s,\bar{X}(s),u)-\bar{b}(s)]+\hbox{tr$\,$}\big[\Theta_{xx}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))[a(s,\bar{X}(s),u)-\bar{a}(s)]\big]\Big]ds

\displaystyle\quad-{1\over 2}\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\hbox{tr$\,$}\Big[\Theta^{0}_{yy}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s)))\Theta_{x}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{\sigma}(s)[\Theta_{x}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{\sigma}(s)]^{\top}\Big]ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\Big[g^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s,u)-\bar{g}^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s)+\Theta_{x}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))[b(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)-\bar{b}^{\varepsilon}(s)]

\displaystyle\quad+\hbox{tr$\,$}\big\{\Theta_{xx}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))[a(s,X^{\varepsilon}(s),u)-\bar{a}^{\varepsilon}(s)]\big\}\Big]ds

\displaystyle\quad+{1\over 2}\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\hbox{tr$\,$}\big[\Theta^{0}_{yy}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta(s,\bar{X}(s)))\Theta_{x}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{\sigma}(s)[\Theta_{x}(s,\bar{X}(s))\bar{\sigma}(s)]^{\top}\big]ds

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\Theta^{0}_{y}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta(s,\bar{X}(s)))\bar{g}(s,\bar{\Psi}(s,\bar{X}(s)))ds\Big\}.

(6.25)

By the standard results of SDEs, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big[\sup_{s\in[t,t+\varepsilon]}\big(|\bar{X}(s)|^{2}+|X^{\varepsilon}(s)|^{2}\big)\Big]\leqslant K(1+|\bar{X}(t)|^{2}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big[\sup_{s\in[t,t+\varepsilon]}|\bar{X}(s)-X^{\varepsilon}(s)|^{2}\Big]\leqslant K(1+|\bar{X}(t)|^{2})\varepsilon.

(6.26)

By
6.3
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[|\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))-\Theta(s,\bar{X}(s))|+|\Theta_{x}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))-\Theta_{x}(s,\bar{X}(s))|

\displaystyle\quad+|\Theta_{xx}^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s))-\Theta_{xx}(s,\bar{X}(s))|\big]\leqslant K\varepsilon^{\alpha\over 2}.

(6.27)

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big[\big|\Theta^{0}_{y}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s)))-\Theta^{0}_{y}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta(s,\bar{X}(s)))\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\big|\Theta^{0}_{yy}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{X}(s)))-\Theta^{0}_{yy}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta(s,\bar{X}(s)))\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\big|\Theta_{x}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))-\Theta_{x}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(s),\Theta(t,\bar{X}(t)))\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\big|\Theta_{xx}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),X^{\varepsilon}(s),\Theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{X}(t)))-\Theta_{xx}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(s),\Theta(t,\bar{X}(t)))\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\leqslant K\varepsilon^{\alpha\over 2}+K\varepsilon^{1\over 2}(1+|\bar{X}(t)|)\leqslant K\varepsilon^{\alpha\over 2}(1+|\bar{X}(t)|),

(6.28)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big[|g^{\varepsilon}(s,u)-\bar{g}(s,u)|+|\bar{g}^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s)-\bar{g}^{0}(t,s,\bar{\Psi}(s,\bar{X}(s)))|

\displaystyle\quad+|\bar{b}^{\varepsilon}(s)-\bar{b}(s)|+|\bar{\sigma}^{\varepsilon}(s)-\bar{\sigma}(s)|\Big]\leqslant K\varepsilon^{\alpha\over 2}(1+|\bar{X}(t)|),

(6.29)

where

\displaystyle\bar{g}^{0}(t,s,u)

\displaystyle:=g^{0}\Big(t,\,s,\,\bar{X}(t),\,\bar{X}(s),\,u,\,\Theta(s,\bar{X}(s)),\,\Theta_{x}(s,\bar{X}(s))\sigma(s,\bar{X}(s),u),

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\Theta^{0}(s,s,\bar{X}(s),\bar{X}(s),\Theta(s,\bar{X}(s))),\,\Theta_{x}^{0}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(s),\Theta(t,\bar{X}(t)))\sigma(s,\bar{X}(s),u)\Big),

(6.30)

and the form of other functions is given in (
6.18
), (
6.21
), and (
6.24
).
Moreover, by
6.3
and (
6.26
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big[|g^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s,u)-\bar{g}^{0}(t,s,u)|\Big]\leqslant K\varepsilon^{\alpha\over 2}(1+|\bar{X}(t)|),

(6.31)

where
g^{0,\varepsilon}(t,s,u)
is given by (
6.16
).
With the above estimates (
6.26
)–(
6.31
), from (
6.25
) we have

\displaystyle Y^{0,\varepsilon}(t)-\bar{Y}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big\{\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\Theta^{0}_{y}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta(s,\bar{X}(s)))\Big[\bar{g}(s,u)-\bar{g}(s,\bar{\Psi}(s,\bar{X}(s)))

\displaystyle\qquad+\Theta_{x}(s,\bar{X}(s))[b(s,\bar{X}(s),u)-\bar{b}(s)]+\hbox{tr$\,$}\big\{\Theta_{xx}(s,\bar{X}(s))[a(s,\bar{X}(s),u)-\bar{a}(s)]\big\}\Big]ds

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{t}^{t+\varepsilon}\Big[\bar{g}^{0}(t,s,u)-\bar{g}(t,s,\bar{\Psi}(s,\bar{X}(s)))+\Theta^{0}_{x}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(s),\Theta(s,\bar{X}(s)))

\displaystyle\qquad\times[b(s,\bar{X}(s),u)-\bar{b}(s)]+\hbox{tr$\,$}\big\{\Theta^{0}_{xx}(t,s,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(s),\Theta(s,\bar{X}(s)))[a(s,\bar{X}(s),u)-\bar{a}(s)]\big\}\Big]ds\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad+o(\varepsilon)(1+|\bar{X}(t)|).

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathop{\underline{\rm lim}}_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}{J(t,\bar{X}(t);\Psi^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))-J(t,\bar{X}(t);\bar{\Psi}(\cdot))\over\varepsilon}=\mathop{\underline{\rm lim}}_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}{Y^{0,\varepsilon}(t)-\bar{Y}^{0}(t)\over\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\quad=\bar{\Theta}^{0}_{y}(t)\Big\{\bar{\Theta}_{x}(t)[b(t,\bar{X}(t),u)-\bar{b}(t)]+\hbox{tr$\,$}\big\{\bar{\Theta}_{xx}(t)[a(t,\bar{X}(t),u)-\bar{a}(t)]\big\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bar{g}(t,t,u)-\bar{g}(t,t,\bar{\Psi}(t,\bar{X}(t)))\Big\}+\bar{\Theta}^{0}_{x}(t)[b(t,\bar{X}(t),u)-\bar{b}(t)]

\displaystyle\qquad+\hbox{tr$\,$}\big\{\bar{\Theta}^{0}_{xx}(t)[a(t,\bar{X}(t),u)-\bar{a}(t)]\big\}+\bar{g}^{0}(t,t,u)-\bar{g}(t,t,\bar{\Psi}(t,\bar{X}(t))),

where

\displaystyle\bar{\Theta}(t):=\Theta(t,\bar{X}(t)),\quad\bar{\Theta}^{0}(t):=\Theta^{0}(t,t,\bar{X}(t),\bar{X}(t),\Theta(t,\bar{X}(t))),\quad t\in[0,T].

Then by the local optimality condition (
2.17
) of
\bar{\Psi}(\cdot,\cdot)
, we have

\mathop{\underline{\rm lim}}_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}{J(t,\bar{X}(t);\Psi^{\varepsilon}(\cdot))-J(t,\bar{X}(t);\bar{\Psi}(\cdot))\over\varepsilon}\geqslant 0,

which completes the proof.

7
Some Proofs

7.1
Proof of
Theorem 2.10

For the ease of presentation, in the rest of the paper we restrict to the case with
m=1
only.
However, all our results hold true in the multiple dimensional situation.
To begin with, let us first adopt some notations.

Some Notations:

For any functions
\varsigma:[S,T]\to\mathbb{R}
and
\nu:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
, with
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
S\in[0,T)
, let

\|\varsigma(\cdot)\|_{\alpha\over 2}=\sup_{s_{1},s_{2}\in[S,T],\,s_{1}\neq s_{2}}{|\varsigma(s_{1})-\varsigma(s_{2})|\over|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\alpha\over 2}},\quad\|\nu(\cdot)\|_{\alpha}=\sup_{x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\,x_{1}\neq x_{2}}{|\nu(x_{1})-\nu(x_{2})|\over|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha}}.

For any
\varphi:[S,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
, let

\displaystyle\|\varphi(\cdot\,,\cdot)\|_{C^{{\alpha\over 2},\alpha}([S,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle=\|\varphi(\cdot\,,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}([S,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R})}+\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\|\varphi(\cdot\,,x)\|_{\alpha\over 2}+\sup_{s\in[S,T]}\|\varphi(s,\cdot)\|_{\alpha},

\displaystyle\|\varphi(\cdot\,,\cdot)\|_{C^{{\alpha\over 2},1+\alpha}([S,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle=\|\varphi(\cdot\,,\cdot)\|_{C^{0,1}([S,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R})}+\|\varphi(\cdot\,,\cdot)\|_{C^{{\alpha\over 2},\alpha}([S,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\varphi_{x}(\cdot\,,\cdot)\|_{C^{{\alpha\over 2},\alpha}([S,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R})}.

We will often simply write
C^{{\alpha\over 2},1+\alpha}([S,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R})
as
C^{{\alpha\over 2},1+\alpha}
when there is no confusion.
Similarly, we can define
C^{{\alpha\over 2},{\alpha\over 2},\alpha,1+\alpha,2}([S,T]\times[S,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
, etc.

For any
\theta(\cdot)\in C^{{\alpha\over 2},1+\alpha}
and
\theta^{0}(\cdot)\in C^{{\alpha\over 2},{\alpha\over 2},\alpha,1+\alpha,2}
,
let us consider the following PDE:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Theta_{s}(s,x)+{\cal L}[s;\theta(\cdot),\theta^{0}(\cdot)]\Theta(s,x)+\tilde{g}\big(s,x,\theta(s,x),\theta_{x}(s,x),\theta^{0}(s,x,s,x,\theta(s,x)),\\
&\quad\theta_{x}^{0}(s,x,s,x,\theta(s,x)),\theta_{y}^{0}(s,x,s,x,\theta(s,x))\big)=0,\\
&\Theta^{0}_{s}(t,s,\tilde{x},x,y)+{\cal L}[s;\theta(\cdot),\theta^{0}(\cdot)]\Theta^{0}(t,s,\tilde{x},x,y)+\tilde{g}^{0}\big(t,s,\tilde{x},x,\theta(s,x),\theta_{x}(s,x),\\
&\quad\theta^{0}(s,x,s,x,\theta(s,x)),\theta_{x}^{0}(s,x,s,x,\theta(s,x)),\theta_{y}^{0}(s,x,s,x,\theta(s,x)),\Theta^{0}_{x}(t,s,\tilde{x},x,y)\big)=0,\\
&\Theta(T,x)=h(x),\quad\Theta^{0}_{s}(t,T,\tilde{x},x,y)=h^{0}(t,\tilde{x},x,y),\end{aligned}\right.

(7.1)

where the differential operator
{\cal L}[s;\theta(\cdot),\theta^{0}(\cdot)]
is defined by the following:

\displaystyle{\cal L}[s;\theta(\cdot),\theta^{0}(\cdot)]\varphi(x)

\displaystyle=\hbox{tr$\,$}[\varphi_{xx}(x)a(s,x)]+\varphi_{x}(x)\tilde{b}\big(s,x,\theta(s,x),\theta_{x}(s,x),\theta^{0}(s,x,s,x,\theta(s,x)),

\displaystyle\quad\,\theta^{0}_{x}(s,x,s,x,\theta(s,x)),\theta^{0}_{y}(s,x,s,x,\theta(s,x))\big),\quad\forall\varphi(\cdot)\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R}).

(7.2)

We first present a result for the well-posedness of PDE (
7.1
).

Lemma 7.1
.

Fix a
(t,\tilde{x},y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
.
Then for any
\theta(\cdot)\in C^{{\alpha\over 2},1+\alpha}
and
\theta^{0}(\cdot)\in C^{{\alpha\over 2},{\alpha\over 2},\alpha,1+\alpha,2}
,
the PDE (
7.1
) admits a unique classical solution
(\Theta(\cdot,\cdot),\Theta^{0}(t,\cdot,\tilde{x},\cdot,y))\in C^{1+{\alpha\over 2},2+\alpha}\times C^{1+{\alpha\over 2},2+\alpha}
. Moreover, the following relationship holds:

\displaystyle\Theta(s,x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Xi(s,x,T,\mu)h(\mu)d\mu+\int_{s}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Xi(s,x,r,\mu)\Big[\Theta_{x}(r,\mu)

\displaystyle\quad\times\tilde{b}\big(r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu),\theta_{x}(r,\mu),\theta^{0}(r,\mu,r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu)),\theta_{x}^{0}(r,\mu,r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu)),\theta_{y}^{0}(r,\mu,r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu))\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\tilde{g}\big(r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu),\theta_{x}(r,\mu),\theta^{0}(r,\mu,r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu)),\theta_{x}^{0}(r,\mu,r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu)),\theta_{y}^{0}(r,\mu,r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu))\big)\Big]d\mu dr;

(7.3)

\displaystyle\Theta^{0}(t,s,\tilde{x},x,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Xi(s,x,T,\mu)L(t,\tilde{x},\mu,y)d\mu+\int_{s}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Xi(s,x,r,\mu)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big[\Theta^{0}_{x}(t,r,\tilde{x},\mu,y)\tilde{b}\big(r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu),\theta_{x}(r,\mu),\theta^{0}(r,\mu,r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu)),\theta_{x}^{0}(r,\mu,r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu)),

\displaystyle\qquad\theta_{y}^{0}(r,\mu,r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu))\big)+\tilde{g}^{0}\big(t,r,\tilde{x},\mu,\theta(r,\mu),\theta_{x}(r,\mu),\theta^{0}(r,\mu,r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu)),

\displaystyle\qquad\theta_{x}^{0}(r,\mu,r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu)),\theta_{y}^{0}(r,\mu,r,\mu,\theta(r,\mu)),\Theta^{0}_{x}(t,r,\tilde{x},\mu,y)\big)\Big]d\mu dr,

(7.4)

where
\Xi(\cdot\,,\cdot\,,\cdot\,,\cdot)
is given by the following explicitly:

\displaystyle\Xi(s,x,r,\mu)={1\over(4\pi(r-s))^{{n\over 2}}(\hbox{det\,}[a(r,\mu)])^{{1\over 2}}}e^{-{\langle a(r,\mu)^{-1}(x-\mu),(x-\mu)\rangle\over 4(r-s)}},\quad(s,x),(r,\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(7.5)

Proof.

For any fixed
\theta(\cdot)\in C^{{\alpha\over 2},1+\alpha}
and
\theta^{0}(\cdot)\in C^{{\alpha\over 2},{\alpha\over 2},\alpha,1+\alpha,2}
, denote

\displaystyle\nu_{1}(s,x)=\theta(s,x),\quad\nu_{2}(s,x)=\theta_{x}(s,x),\quad\nu_{3}(s,x)=\theta^{0}(s,s,x,x,\theta(s,x)),

\displaystyle\nu_{4}(s,x)=\theta_{x}^{0}(s,s,x,x,\theta(s,x)),\quad\nu_{5}(s,x)=\theta_{y}^{0}(s,s,x,x,\theta(s,x)),\quad(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Then we have
\nu_{i}(\cdot)\in C^{{\alpha\over 2},\alpha}
, for
i=1,...,5
.
Taking
(t,\tilde{x},y)
as parameters, by the standard results of parabolic equations
(see
[
23
, Theorem 12, Chapter 1]
or
[
36
, Chapter IV, Sections 13–14]
, for example),
we get that PDE (
7.1
) admits a unique classical solution and (
7.3
)–(
7.4
) hold.
∎

Direct computations show that (see
[
23
,
36
]
, for example)
